16:I[12454,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0otjhi71vbmnx.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js"],"WeeklyView"]
17:T48f,Daedalus-150M is a convolution–attention hybrid decoder. Out of 18 blocks, only 6 are full attention; the other 12 are short convolution blocks with kernel length L=3 depthwise conv and a fixed recurrent state of width L-1=2 timesteps, making their per-token decode cost independent of context length. Attention layers are interleaved across depth (a fixed C/A pattern) to preserve long-range retrieval at multiple representation levels. To further reduce cache traffic, attention uses grouped-query attention (12 Q heads, 4 KV heads). The model ties input/output embeddings to cut parameters and memory traffic, and uses a narrower FFN (2048, ~2.67×d_model) to reduce bandwidth-heavy matrices. The paper explicitly documents rejected options (MoE due to export/runtime constraints and lack of sub-1B precedent; position-free attention due to export format; distillation due to teacher-logit storage cost). A first-order CPU decode cost model is provided: per token, read quantized weights once plus KV-cache reads proportional to context length for each attention layer; this yields an analytic cache coefficient κ used to predict hybrid vs dense decoding costs.18:T491,The analysis is set in the continuous-time Markov chain formulation of discrete diffusion. A transition-rate matrix defines the forward noising process and a family of marginal distributions p_t. The reverse process requires concrete scores, i.e. pairwise ratios p_t(y)/p_t(x). SEDD learns these ratios with the score-entropy loss, a weighted Bregman divergence D(a,b)=a/b-1-log(a/b), where the weights are induced by the transition structure.

The main contribution is a minimax treatment of this estimation problem. The paper first derives a lower bound for estimating a single concrete score function under the score-entropy risk. It then constructs a simple estimator, essentially a smoothed or clipped empirical plug-in estimator for the relevant probability ratios, and proves that its risk nearly matches the lower bound. The single-time result is then connected back to SEDD by controlling the time-averaged score-estimation term that appears in KL guarantees for the generated distribution. This complements analyses of tau-leaping, uniformization, Gillespie, or Euler samplers, which study how to simulate the reverse chain once accurate scores are available.19:T4bb,A sampling-based watermark adapted to diffusion decoding.
- Injection: replace position-indexed i.i.d. Gumbel perturbations with a smooth, locally auto-correlated Gumbel field (SAC). The field is constructed via a Gaussian copula so that each coordinate keeps Gumbel marginals (under the paper’s assumptions) while introducing controlled local dependence across neighboring positions.
- Rationale: diffusion decoding refines multiple positions in parallel; independent high-frequency perturbations across neighbors can conflict with local consistency during iterative updates, causing oscillations or incoherence.
- Mechanistic analysis: quantify “latent perturbation roughness” via a local-difference energy and show how positive low-lag dependence reduces this roughness (the paper provides an analytic form contrasting i.i.d. vs SAC).
- Detection: a SAC-aware detector using covariance-aware filtering plus native-sample calibration to manage false positives. A global-offset scan is added as a coarse synchronization recovery tool under mild token–signal misalignment.
Overall, it modifies keyed randomness during DLM decoding and pairs it with a matched detector, rather than changing DLM training.1a:T449,The diagnostic pair is a Qwen3.5-27B mentor and a Qwen3.5-4B student. On Multi-IF, linear probes on mentor states show a large freshness gap: stale-state accuracy falls from 0.79 at t=0 to 0.47 at t=512, while fresh-state accuracy stays between 0.76 and 0.80. This drift also affects behavior. Static guidance reduces the 4B student’s constraint satisfaction by 2.5 points below the no-guidance baseline, while refreshing every 16 tokens turns it into a 2.0-point gain over that baseline. Fresh but mismatched memory does not help, which supports the content-freshness explanation. Across 13 datasets, MentorPulse closes 52.2% of the mentor-student gap on the main pair, roughly twice the recovery of the strongest prior alternative. Gains concentrate on long-output tasks. Short outputs often finish before refresh matters, and very large mentor-student capability gaps reduce the margin. The proposed V64 read-distribution indicator helps screen pairs before deployment. Throughput measurements place R=16 among the efficient settings tested against text-to-text guidance on long-output tasks.1b:T820,方法延续 FlashPrefill 的两阶段思路。先做 block-level 的粗粒度打分，再只对被选中的 block 做精细 attention。原始 FlashPrefill 用 probe queries 和 block mean-pooled key 来估计每个 key block 的重要性，并用基于峰值分数的动态阈值替代 Top-k/Top-p 排序，从而避免全局排序开销。它还保留 sink blocks、局部滑窗和最近块，以兼顾因果约束和常见注意力模式。

V2 的第一个关键改动是 mean correction term。原方法在极端稀疏时直接截断未选 block 的 softmax 分母和分子，忽略掉被剪枝块的质量，误差会迅速放大。V2 为每个 key block 预先计算 pooled K/V 统计量，用零阶近似去补偿被丢弃块对 softmax mass 和 value aggregation 的贡献。直观上，它不再把未选 block 当成完全不存在，而是用块均值代表其剩余贡献。这样做的好处是代价低，不需要恢复 token-level 计算，但能显著缓和高 sparsity 下的偏差。

第二个关键改动是 kernel 重写，目标是对齐 Hopper 时代的高性能 dense attention 实现。文中提到三点：PackGQA memory access、warp specialization、pingpong pipelining。前者针对 grouped-query attention 的访存组织，减少无效带宽和访存碎片；warp specialization 把不同 warp 分配给不同职责，提高流水并行；pingpong pipeline 则利用 Hopper 上的异步搬运和计算重叠机制，把数据准备和计算交替推进。作者明确说 V2 是按 FlashAttention-3/4 的实现思路重构，而不是停留在 FA2 时代的 kernel 结构上。

第三个关键改动是系统兼容性。V2 原生支持 paged KV cache 和 continuous batching，并集成到 SGLang 这类现代推理框架中。这个点很重要，因为很多 academic sparse attention 方法默认 contiguous KV layout，离真实 serving 很远；一旦接入 paged cache，索引、页表、batch 动态拼接都会改变 kernel 设计。V2 把这一层一起处理了，所以论文不只是算子 benchmark，而是端到端 TTFT 改善。1c:T8b0,FlashPrefill V2 keeps the two-stage structure of FlashPrefill. It first performs block-level coarse scoring, then computes fine-grained attention only on selected blocks. The original method uses probe queries and mean-pooled keys to estimate block salience, and replaces Top-k/Top-p sorting with a max-based dynamic threshold. It also preserves sink blocks, a local sliding window, and recent blocks to respect causal structure and common attention patterns.

The first major addition in V2 is a mean correction term. In the original truncation-based approximation, unselected blocks are simply dropped from both the softmax denominator and numerator. Under extreme sparsity, that discarded probability mass becomes too large to ignore. V2 compensates for pruned blocks using precomputed pooled K/V statistics for each block, effectively adding back a zero-order surrogate contribution. Intuitively, the method no longer treats pruned blocks as nonexistent; it represents their residual effect through block means. This keeps the correction cheap while significantly reducing approximation error at high sparsity.

The second major change is a Hopper-oriented kernel redesign. The paper highlights PackGQA memory access, warp specialization, and pingpong pipelining. PackGQA improves memory organization for grouped-query attention and reduces bandwidth waste. Warp specialization assigns different roles to different warps to improve overlap and utilization. Pingpong pipelining exploits Hopper-style asynchronous data movement and compute overlap. The authors explicitly position V2 as aligned with FlashAttention-3/4 implementation principles rather than older FA2-style kernels.

The third change is systems compatibility. V2 natively supports paged KV cache and continuous batching, and is integrated into SGLang-like serving frameworks. This is important because many academic sparse-attention methods assume contiguous KV layouts and do not survive contact with real serving systems. Once paged cache, page tables, and dynamic batching enter the picture, the kernel design changes materially. V2 addresses that layer directly, so the contribution is not just an operator benchmark but an end-to-end TTFT improvement.1d:T620,The empirical story has three parts: speed, quality, and deployability. On NVIDIA H20 at 128K context in BF16, FlashPrefill V2 reports up to 27.19x speedup over FlashAttention-2 during prefill. Its FP8 variant reaches 47.26x. More importantly, even against a much stronger dense baseline aligned with FlashAttention-3/4, it still achieves 17.54x speedup in BF16 and 30.49x in FP8. That means the gain is not an artifact of comparing against an outdated dense kernel. The paper also notes that acceleration remains visible even at 4K sequence length, suggesting that sparse selection and launch overheads are kept low enough to matter outside only ultra-long contexts.

At the system level, after integration into SGLang, the method reduces end-to-end time-to-first-token by up to 4.8x at 128K with only marginal accuracy loss. This result is more convincing than raw kernel throughput because it includes the effects of paged KV cache, continuous batching, and framework-level scheduling.

On accuracy, the headline claim is that mean correction keeps degradation manageable even under extreme sparsity. The method section explains why: naive truncation drops the softmax mass of pruned blocks, while the correction restores an approximation of that contribution using block-level statistics. The excerpt provided here does not include the full downstream quality tables or exact error curves, but the intended contribution is clear: not zero-loss sparsification, but a much better quality-speed tradeoff in the high-sparsity regime where deployment actually benefits.1e:T4d8,Read this paper with a systems lens. Start from the introduction and keep the three prototype gaps in mind: approximation quality, kernel efficiency, and serving compatibility. Then go straight to the mean-correction section and understand why it restores pruned softmax mass. You do not need to follow every algebraic detail, but you should recognize that this is a cheap zero-order block-level correction rather than a heavier higher-order scheme. After that, read the kernel-design section carefully, especially how it maps to Hopper execution behavior and why PackGQA, warp specialization, and pingpong pipelining appear together. Finally, spend time on the integration and end-to-end results, not only the operator microbenchmarks.

If you work on long-context serving, the most useful figures are the speed curves across context lengths, precisions, and baselines, plus the accuracy-versus-sparsity tradeoff. If your focus is more on pretraining, the main takeaway is simpler: many long-context ideas look good at the algorithm level, but deployable context scaling often depends on whether the kernel and serving interface are co-designed. A solid skim should take 30–45 minutes; a reproduction-oriented read will take much longer.1f:T655,FleetSieve treats profiling as downstream decision identification. For each workload-class/configuration cell, it maintains intervals for sustainable capacity and tail latency rather than a single estimate. It then solves two fleet allocations under the same resource constraints and lexicographic objective: a conservative allocation using lower capacity and upper tail bounds, and an optimistic allocation using upper capacity and lower tail bounds. The objective first enforces minimum fulfillment floors for critical classes when feasible, then maximizes the minimum satisfaction ratio, then total served goodput, and finally reduces fragmentation.

The key idea is the acquisition rule. FleetSieve does not measure the cell with the largest uncertainty, nor does it try to cover the grid uniformly. It measures the configuration expected to reduce disagreement between the conservative and optimistic downstream allocations. In other words, a cell with high uncertainty is not important if no plausible outcome would change the fleet decision. Conversely, a cell becomes important if resolving it could flip SLO feasibility or alter resource allocation for a critical workload.

The paper also introduces a conditional decision certificate with three states: Certified-Feasible, Undecided, and Certified-Infeasible. The wording is careful. The certificate is not distribution-free or unconditional; it is valid only under the empirical uncertainty model used to bound the unknown performance table. That restraint is a good sign: the method is presented as an engineering decision tool, not as a universal guarantee.20:T491,The evaluation is intentionally narrow and concrete: one 31B open-weight decoder model, one H100 node, FP8, a vLLM-based serving stack, speculative decoding disabled, and candidate TP degrees {2, 4, 8}. Each profiling cell is measured for 300 seconds. The main metric is how many profiling GPU-seconds are needed to recover the oracle aggregate fleet decision.

The headline result is modest but real: on the fixed H100 measurement grid, FleetSieve reaches the oracle aggregate decision with 22,200 GPU-seconds, a 6.9% reduction relative to uniform profiling. The paper is refreshingly explicit that gains are selective rather than universal: the reduction is larger for Chat and absent for Code. That matters because it clarifies when the method helps. If unresolved cells can still change the global allocation, decision-aware profiling is useful. If the allocation is effectively determined early, the extra machinery may not buy much. The experiments also show why joint modeling of capacity and tail latency is necessary: throughput-only profiling can certify configurations that look efficient but violate the actual SLO, which then corrupts fleet-level planning.21:T584,CacheRoute builds a periodically refreshed routing table that plans prefix affinity and expected load together, prioritizing cache stability over per-request reactive remapping.
- Inputs/abstraction: per-key arrival rates λ_b for business keys; R routable destinations (a destination is a model-server group; in the 70B setup, TP=2).
- Admission (warm set): admit the highest-rate keys into an affinity-managed warm set under a warm-prefix slot budget; non-admitted traffic falls back to cache-blind power-of-two choices.
- Load cap via replication: calibrate q_cap from a single destination’s latency/load knee. For each key, allocate enough destinations so the expected per-destination load contributed by that key is ≤ q_cap. Replication is primarily for load control (capping per-destination contribution), not for improving hit rate per se.
- Placement: assign admitted keys (and their replica counts) to destinations using LPT (longest-processing-time-first) list scheduling to reduce expected load imbalance.
- Control interval: keep the table fixed for a period to preserve cache warmth; avoid reactive spilling that would send the next request to a cold destination once a cap binds.
The novelty is not LPT or top-k admission individually, but the combined routing design and the empirical characterization of when planned affinity beats both cache-blind balancing and reactive cache-aware routing.22:T7ab,方法分成两部分：TDES 和 SABA。

TDES（Target-Distilled Edge Scoring）解决父子错配。现有 DDTree 的边选择近似是 parent-agnostic 的：child token 在不同 parent 下贡献相同，只由该位置的 token probability 决定。GRAFT 改成显式给 edge 打分。做法是从 target model 的验证轨迹里蒸馏一个轻量 edge scorer，输入是相邻位置候选节点及其上下文特征，输出该 parent-child transition 被 target 接受的兼容性分数。然后树构建时不再只按各位置 token 概率乘积选路径，而是结合 edge score 进行 parent-conditional 组装。这样保留了 DLM 一次前向产出候选节点的优点，又补上了“节点之间是否能连成 target 偏好的路径”这一层信息。

从机制上看，这一步很像把 target 的局部条件分布偏好压缩成一个便宜的结构预测器。它不是重新跑 target，也不是让 drafter 变成 AR，而是在 assembly 阶段补一个 learned compatibility model。相比直接增大候选集，这种做法更有效，因为问题不是候选 token 不在，而是树结构把它们接错了。

SABA（State-Aware Budget Allocation）解决固定预算失配。作者把每轮 throughput 看成 accepted gain 与 verification overhead 的函数，学习一个按当前 decoding state 选择 tree budget 的策略。直觉上，easy round 本来 acceptance 就长，再加节点收益很快饱和；hard round 适度扩树更值钱，但也不是越大越好。SABA 用当前轮的状态特征预测不同 budget 下的收益，选 throughput 最优预算。这里的关键不是复杂控制器，而是把“树大小”从静态超参变成在线决策变量。

整体上，GRAFT 没有改 DLM 本身，也没有改 target verification 机制，而是专门优化 DLM-based tree assembly 这一层。这种切分是合理的，因为瓶颈正出在 one-pass future distributions 到 executable tree 的映射。23:T781,GRAFT has two components: TDES and SABA.

TDES, or Target-Distilled Edge Scoring, addresses parent-child mismatch. DDTree is effectively parent-agnostic: a child token contributes the same score regardless of which parent path it extends, because scoring is driven by per-position token probabilities. GRAFT instead learns an explicit edge scorer distilled from target-model traces. The scorer takes candidate parent-child pairs from adjacent positions, together with contextual features, and predicts how compatible that transition is with the target model. Tree assembly then uses these edge scores, rather than only the product of marginal token probabilities, to select target-compatible paths under a node budget.

Mechanistically, this is a lightweight structural predictor that compresses local target preferences into a cheap model. It does not rerun the target and does not force the DLM drafter into autoregressive expansion. It patches the missing conditional dependency at the tree-assembly stage. That is the right intervention point, because the failure mode is not merely missing candidate tokens; it is incorrect attachment among already available candidates.

SABA, or State-Aware Budget Allocation, addresses budget misallocation. The paper models per-round throughput as a trade-off between draft gain and verification cost, then chooses the budget conditioned on the current decoding state. Easy rounds already have long accepted prefixes, so larger trees mostly add cost. Hard rounds benefit more from extra nodes, but only up to a saturation point. SABA predicts the utility of different budgets from state features and selects the throughput-optimal one online.

Overall, GRAFT leaves the DLM and the target verification mechanism unchanged. It focuses specifically on the mapping from one-pass future distributions to an executable draft tree. That decomposition is sensible and technically clean.24:T4a2,The main empirical claim is consistent end-to-end throughput improvement with very small overhead. The paper reports 2.13x to 6.36x throughput gains over baselines across models and datasets, while adding less than 0.5 ms overhead per decoding round. That matters because it suggests the method is not trading away the cost structure of speculative decoding.

The ablations are more informative than the headline numbers. TDES improves MAT, indicating that parent-child compatibility is a real bottleneck for DLM-based tree construction. The paper shows both examples and aggregate evidence that target-compatible tokens are often already present among candidates, but become unusable because they are connected to the wrong parent. SABA shows that a fixed budget is not robust: the throughput-optimal tree size varies substantially across rounds. Dynamic allocation preserves most of the acceptance gain while avoiding unnecessary verification cost.

From a systems perspective, the strongest result is not a single benchmark number but the combination of stable gains across tasks and low added latency. That makes the method more credible as a practical layer on top of DLM drafters.25:T455,This is a skim paper, but the introduction, method section, and ablations are worth reading carefully.

Focus on three things. First, the two failure modes defined in the introduction: parent-child mismatch and budget misallocation. If those are convincing, the rest of the paper follows naturally. Second, inspect how TDES gets supervision from target traces and what features the edge scorer actually uses. The important question is what information is added beyond marginal token probabilities. Third, check whether SABA is optimizing throughput directly or only a proxy such as acceptance length.

For figures and tables, prioritize the mismatch examples, the MAT/throughput comparisons, and the curves showing diminishing returns with larger budgets. Expected reading time is about 25 to 35 minutes. If reading selectively, the problem setup, the two modules, and the ablations are enough. If reading more deeply, two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: how target-specific the distilled edge scorer is, and whether the budget policy can generalize to broader adaptive verification strategies.26:T4ff,The method, DPQ (Doubt-Preserving Quantization), is a family of pre-quantization calibration-data recipes rather than a new quantizer. The pipeline is lightweight. First, run the full-precision model on candidate examples. Then select target-aware 'fragile' examples using FP uncertainty signals. Two signal families matter most: high-doubt examples, such as high entropy, low confidence, or small top-2 margin; and answerability-boundary examples, especially for answer/no-answer settings like SQuAD2. These are mixed with generic anchor examples and then passed to an unchanged quantizer such as GPTQ or AWQ. The theoretical argument is modest but useful: if the deployment target puts more mass near decision boundaries, while the calibration mixture is dominated by generic anchors, the optimal calibration set will differ from the one preferred by broad MCQA-style targets. The paper calls this mixture mismatch. The framing is built around two preservation risks: a distributional risk that measures deviation between quantized and FP predictive distributions, and a boundary risk that measures decision flips in low-margin regions, optionally reweighted toward answerability or abstention-heavy inputs. This is a better fit for real systems than accuracy-only calibration.27:T539,The evaluation spans 8 language models, 9 NLP benchmarks, and 22 comparison methods or configurations. The metric suite is broader than standard quantization papers: besides ECE, adaptive ECE, NLL, and Brier score, the authors report FP-behavior metrics such as FP agreement, JSD between quantized and FP predictive distributions, confidence shift, top-two margin drift, and margin correlation. On SQuAD2 they also evaluate answerability-boundary behavior directly. Three findings stand out. First, the best calibration recipe depends strongly on the target. For boundary-sensitive answerability preservation, a high-boundary mixture such as DPQ-s128-r75 works best. For broad MCQA, milder recipes such as DPQ-r50 or single-signal confidence-only / entropy-only selection are often better. Second, accuracy can hide substantial uncertainty drift: some settings preserve top-1 behavior while materially changing confidence and margin structure. Third, preservation signals transfer only partially across quantizers. The post-hoc calibration and AWQ analyses suggest that some useful signals are not tied to one quantizer, but the best score-preservation recipe still remains quantizer-specific. The paper is less about one headline average gain and more about establishing the robustness of this target dependence across models and tasks.28:T758,系统结果是全文最硬的部分。作者在 NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU 上报告，优化后的 BF16 实现从 4K 上下文开始超过 dense attention；在 32K 时，单层 attention 达到 10.91× speedup。更重要的是，真实的八层 retrofit 在 warm 32K whole-model TTFT 上带来 15.3% 的下降。这个数字没有单层 10× 那么夸张，但更可信，因为它把 attention 之外的层、调度、kernel launch、planning 等开销都算进去了。

训练结果方面，论文做了三种 matched Stage A 对照：Dense continued training、equal-pair-count local sliding、equal-pair-count static-random nonlocal graph。BF1 在三次训练 seed 下都排第一。这里最关键的不是“第一”这个标签，而是控制做得比较细：local sliding 和 random graph 都匹配了 pair budget，因此 BF1 的优势更可能来自拓扑结构，而不是单纯看得更多。换句话说，作者试图回答的是“在相同稀疏预算下，dyadic + first-block 这种图是否比局部或随机图更适合 next-token LM”。

论文还单独讨论了 selected-page decode semantics，并指出在低 batch 场景下，主瓶颈不是 selected-page attention arithmetic，而是 planning lifecycle。这个观察很重要，因为它解释了为什么很多稀疏 decode 方案在 paper benchmark 上很快，落到真实 serving 却收益有限：算子省下来的时间被 page selection、mask planning、runtime orchestration 吃掉了。

从当前给出的正文片段看，底座是 Qwen3-0.6B 几何配置。若全文有 perplexity 或 downstream LM 指标，阅读时要重点看 BF1 retrofit 后相对 dense-continued-training 的差距是否稳定很小，以及这种差距是否随上下文长度扩大。如果长上下文收益建立在明显牺牲短上下文 LM 质量之上，那方法的适用面会窄很多。29:T723,BF1 is a deterministic, block-aligned causal sparse-attention pattern. The sequence is partitioned into blocks of width b, with the reported public configuration using b = 64. For a query block j, the route includes four components: the causal self block, two exact local predecessor blocks, the first global block, and historical predecessor blocks at dyadic distances, i.e. j - 2^r starting from an offset of four blocks. In short, it is local + global-first + dyadic history.

The paper is explicit that this route is related to earlier log-sparse and dilated designs such as LogSparse Transformer and LongNet. The claimed novelty lies elsewhere: pretrained-model retrofit, matched topology-control experiments, and correctness-gated Blackwell measurements. The correctness-gated part is important. The authors do not only benchmark a fast path; they verify partial blocks, backward gradients, dense-path equivalence, future-token isolation, and opposite-order benchmarks up to 32K, which makes the systems claims more credible.

With fixed block width, each converted layer uses O(n log n) selected token interactions and has O(log n) graph communication depth. The communication-depth argument is the structural reason BF1 should beat pure local sliding windows: long-range information can propagate in logarithmically many sparse hops rather than linearly many local layers. Compared with static random nonlocal graphs, BF1 is deterministic and easier to optimize at the kernel level.

Another sensible design choice is that the paper does not replace all attention layers in the model and call it done. It reports an actual eight-layer retrofit separately from an all-layer timing-only ceiling. That makes the results more realistic for pretrained models, where replacing every layer with a sparse operator can be risky.2a:T74e,The strongest evidence is on the systems side. On an NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU, the optimized BF16 implementation reportedly crosses dense attention around 4K context length. At 32K, the per-layer attention operator reaches 10.91× speedup. More importantly, the actual eight-layer retrofit reduces warm whole-model TTFT by 15.3% at 32K. That number is much smaller than the per-layer speedup, but it is also much more informative because it includes the rest of the model and runtime overheads.

For language modeling, the paper runs three matched Stage A training comparisons: dense continued training, equal-pair-count local sliding, and equal-pair-count static-random nonlocal graphs. BF1 ranks first in all three matched runs. The key point is not the ranking alone. The controls are designed to keep the sparse budget comparable, so the advantage is more plausibly due to topology rather than simply attending to more pairs. In other words, the paper tries to answer whether a dyadic-plus-first-block graph is a better sparse inductive bias for next-token LM than local-only or random sparse graphs under similar budget.

The decode analysis is also useful. The paper reports selected-page decode semantics and argues that, at low batch, the dominant unresolved cost is planning lifecycle rather than selected-page attention arithmetic. That observation explains why sparse decode methods often look strong in operator benchmarks but deliver limited gains in real serving stacks.

From the provided text, the tested geometry is based on Qwen3-0.6B. When reading the full paper, the most important thing to check is how much LM quality is lost relative to dense continued training, and whether that gap stays small across context lengths. If long-context efficiency comes with a noticeable short-context LM penalty, the deployment envelope becomes narrower.2b:T43b,A good skim order is: operator definition, systems measurements, then matched training controls. Start with the route definition and complexity proposition to understand exactly which blocks are connected and why the selected interactions are O(n log n) with O(log n) communication depth. Then jump to the systems section and focus on the dense crossover point, the 32K per-layer speedup, and the whole-model TTFT for the eight-layer retrofit. Finally, read the matched Stage A training results to see whether topology, sparse budget, and training adaptation are properly disentangled.

If you only have about 25 minutes, prioritize Figure 1, the complexity statement, the correctness-gated Blackwell benchmark tables, and the matched-control training results. Three details are worth deeper inspection: why the first block is used as the global anchor instead of learned global tokens, which eight layers were retrofitted and why, and how the planning-lifecycle overhead is broken down during decode. That last point may be more operationally important than the sparse route itself.2c:T885,方法核心是 trace → execution graph → workload synthesis → deterministic replay → profiling 这一整条链路。

1. 通用 tracing。XPerf 支持对 LangChain、LangGraph 等常见 agent 框架做无侵入或低侵入埋点；对自建 agent 只需很少注释。它记录的不只是 LLM 请求本身，还包括工具调用及其时间信息。

2. 执行图重建。论文用基于 time-span tree 的分析方法，从 traces 中恢复 LLM call、tool call 及其依赖关系，得到 execution graph。这个设计比简单按时间顺序回放更合理，因为 agent 负载里存在并发、等待工具返回、条件分支等结构，只有显式依赖图才能保留真实调度约束。

3. 工作负载合成。作者没有把 benchmark 限定为“原样回放历史 trace”，而是允许在 execution graph 之上改变请求到达模式，构造 online serving、offline batch inference、fixed-concurrency 等不同场景。这一点很关键，因为系统研究需要控制 arrival process，才能比较调度器、batching、cache 策略在不同负载区间的行为。

4. 可复现回放。为保证 replay fidelity，XPerf 对每个 LLM call 提交记录下来的精确 input token IDs，而不是重新 tokenize 文本；这样可以保留 prefix cache 命中行为。必要时还可以强制 serving engine 解码记录过的 output tokens，避免因为模型输出偏移而改变后续控制流。回放时还遵守 execution graph 的依赖顺序，并把工具调用时长纳入时间轴，因此即便被测 serving engine 与 tracing 时不同，负载形态仍尽量贴近原始应用。

5. 细粒度 profiling。系统层面报告 KV cache usage、prefix cache hit rate、token throughput 等；硬件层面报告 GPU tensor core 利用率、memory bandwidth 利用率等。作者强调 profiler 开销很低，避免 benchmark 本身扭曲结果。

和已有工作相比，区别主要在两点：一是它不是只测独立请求，也不是只做 agent 任务完成率；二是它公开、可扩展，并且把“非确定性 agent 行为”通过 trace replay 固定下来，而不是接受 run-to-run variance 作为噪声。2d:T896,The method is a full pipeline: trace collection, execution-graph reconstruction, workload synthesis, deterministic replay, and low-overhead profiling.

1. Application-agnostic tracing. XPerf instruments popular agent frameworks such as LangChain and LangGraph with minimal friction, and supports lightweight annotations for custom agents. It records both LLM calls and tool calls, including timing information.

2. Execution-graph construction. Rather than replaying requests as a flat sequence, XPerf reconstructs dependencies among LLM calls and tool invocations using a time-span-tree-based analysis. This matters because agent workloads contain waiting, branching, and sometimes parallel structure. A dependency graph preserves the scheduling constraints that actually shape serving behavior.

3. Workload synthesis. XPerf does not stop at replaying historical traces. It can alter request arrival patterns over the reconstructed execution graphs to emulate online serving, offline batch inference, and fixed-concurrency settings. This makes the framework useful for controlled systems studies, where arrival processes must be varied independently of application logic.

4. Reproducible replay. For each LLM call, XPerf submits the exact recorded input token IDs, not just the raw text, so prefix-cache behavior is preserved. It can also force the serving engine to decode the recorded output tokens, preventing output drift from changing downstream control flow. Replay respects dependency order in the execution graph and accounts for tool-call durations, so the workload remains realistic even when the target serving engine differs from the one used during trace collection.

5. Fine-grained profiling. XPerf reports serving metrics such as KV-cache usage, prefix-cache hit rate, and token throughput, and hardware metrics such as tensor-core utilization and memory-bandwidth utilization. The profiler is designed to add negligible overhead.

Relative to prior work, the distinction is clear: it is neither an agent-quality benchmark nor a standard serving microbenchmark. Its central idea is to freeze the stochastic control flow of agent applications into a reproducible systems workload.2e:T5aa,The evaluation uses eight agentic applications spanning deep research, coding, Q&A, and general-purpose assistance. The most important results fall into three buckets.

First, replay fidelity. XPerf achieves below 3% mean absolute percentage error in end-to-end request latency for both isolated and concurrent requests. That is the key validation result: if replay were not faithful, the rest of the benchmark would be hard to trust.

Second, workload variance in native agent runs. The paper quantifies how unstable agent execution can be without replay. The headline example is the roughly 70% average variation in the number of LLM calls for the same request under mini-SWE-agent with gpt-oss-120b. This result justifies the entire benchmark design.

Third, bottleneck diagnosis. With replay fixed, XPerf can decompose end-to-end latency and relate it to prefix-cache hits, KV-cache occupancy, token throughput, and GPU compute or bandwidth utilization. The paper’s emphasis is not on claiming one serving engine wins universally, but on showing that the framework can localize where performance is lost under agent-style traffic.

For a foundation-model audience, the useful takeaway is that agent workloads materially change prefix reuse, request-length distributions, and concurrency structure. Optimizations that look effective on standard chat-style benchmarks may behave differently once execution graphs become deeper and more irregular.2f:T46f,The conceptual move is to extend JND from human perception to machine receivers. Instead of asking whether a compressed signal is visually indistinguishable, the paper asks whether the downstream policy response remains effectively unchanged. In a VLA setting, the receiver is a language-conditioned robotic policy and the response is a continuous action vector, including translation, rotation, and gripper control. Compression decisions are therefore guided by action sensitivity rather than by visual similarity or attention mass. Based on the introduction, the method likely defines an action-conditioned noticeability metric for visual tokens or intermediate features, estimates a tolerance boundary or compression budget per token, and uses that budget for pruning, reuse, or feature compression. The main contribution appears more conceptual than architectural: safe compression should be defined by receiver behavior. The exact formulation, how action deviation thresholds are estimated, whether an auxiliary calibration model is used, and where compression is applied in the stack would need the full method section to verify.30:T6ce,The work has two layers. The first is the Thinkingbox sandbox. A task is instantiated as a finite-horizon POMDP whose latent state includes backend state, simulated-user private state, evaluator-relevant event logs, and episode status. At each turn, the agent observes dialogue history and tool outputs, then either sends a user-facing message or issues a tool call with arguments. Tool calls execute inside isolated backend sessions. The system records side effects and runs hidden executable checks at the end. A key design choice is outcome-based evaluation: multiple trajectories may be valid as long as the final backend state, required side effects, and dialogue constraints are correct.

The second layer is Thinkingbox-bench, a 507-task benchmark spanning retail/e-commerce, travel/hospitality, auto insurance, neobank internal IT support, and consulting IT/HR support. Tasks are designed around recurring enterprise patterns: lookup-then-update flows, user clarification, confirmation before irreversible actions, policy-conditioned changes, and multi-tool coordination. Most tasks are graded through executable checks over terminal backend state. An additional subset of 30 tasks uses binary rubrics on the final response, covering required disclosures, confidentiality, and consistency between what the agent says and what it actually executed.

The reliability framing is central. pass@k measures whether at least one of k attempts succeeds, which captures discovery capability. pass^k measures whether all k attempts succeed, which captures repeatable reliability. That distinction is important because many agents can occasionally find a correct trajectory while remaining unstable under sampling noise or interaction variation.31:T56f,The main empirical result is a large discovery–reliability gap. The benchmark contains 507 tasks, and the authors evaluate 12 proprietary and open-weight models with 20 repeated trials per task. The strongest model reaches 65.36% pass@1, which is still far from dependable single-run performance. Its pass@20 rises to 91.12%, showing that many tasks are solvable at least once. But its pass^20 is only 25.25%, meaning only a quarter of tasks succeed in all 20 attempts. That gap is the central takeaway of the paper.

Because evaluation is tied to terminal backend state rather than a single final answer, the benchmark can expose failure modes that simpler tool-use benchmarks miss: updating the wrong entity, producing a convincing response while leaving the backend unchanged, missing a required side effect, introducing an extra side effect, executing high-impact actions before collecting confirmation, or generating a final message inconsistent with the actual execution outcome. The paper also includes evaluator ablations and failure analyses to show that fluent tool use is not the same as reliable work completion.

The provided excerpt does not include the full per-domain or per-model breakdown, but the headline numbers already show strong benchmark discrimination: this is neither plain function-calling nor pure dialogue evaluation; it is outcome-centric stateful execution.32:T461,The method treats response correctness as a binary classification problem. For each query-response pair from a fixed black-box LLM, the feature set includes: (1) one or more existing uncertainty scores, and (2) neighborhood-derived features computed from a labeled reference set. The reference set is embedded, k-nearest neighbors are retrieved for each query, and statistics such as neighbor correctness and cosine similarity are added as features. A lightweight classifier is then trained to predict the probability that the current response is correct.

A key design choice is that the paper does not insist on a single learner. Instead, it compares simple classifiers and concludes that the best learner is task-dependent. This is important: the paper is not claiming a universal parametric confidence model, but a supervised calibration layer over existing UQ signals. Relative to standard zero-shot UQ, the shift is clear: uncertainty scores are treated as imperfect features, not as final confidence estimates. The neighborhood features inject local empirical risk information that zero-shot methods cannot access.33:T42f,The evaluator state is decomposed into three typed sources: grounds for evidence, norms for decision rules, and authority for whether a rule applies to the case. For an old and revised evaluator state, each source can be taken from either version, producing a 2^3 intervention table called the judgment cube. Each hybrid state is executed through a trusted adjudicator. A source replacement set is a reason when it is sufficient to reproduce the revised verdict from the old state, and it is kept only if it is inclusion-minimal. The complete set of such minimal sufficient replacements is the judgment receipt. A key design choice is the separation between prediction and certification: a model can emit a receipt, but only execution of the counterfactual hybrids certifies it. The work also introduces paired consistency as an evaluation primitive. Control transformations carry known metamorphic relations; when a pair violates the relation, at least one prediction must be wrong. This gives an error lower bound without needing gold labels for every transformed item.34:T818,MGAL is built from long-form reports in the UN Digital Library, using position-aligned multilingual documents so that cross-lingual comparisons are not merely task-level but also semantically and structurally aligned. It covers Arabic, Chinese, English, French, Russian, and Spanish, with context lengths from 8K to 128K tokens. The benchmark spans four linguistic granularities—word, sentence, paragraph, and document—and includes both closed-ended and open-ended tasks. The paper mentions seven tasks in total, including word-level QA, sentence-level cloze, paragraph filling, document summarization, and translation, with 420 query-response pairs per task per language.

The key design choice is controlled stratification by evidence position. Examples are labeled by whether the relevant evidence appears at the beginning, middle, or end, indexed at both document and paragraph levels. This turns the benchmark into a diagnostic instrument rather than a generic task suite. The choice of UN reports is practical: they provide multilingual parallelism, topic consistency, and relatively regular discourse structure, which makes coherent unit definitions more reliable.

Quality control is manual and strict. The authors state that every query-response pair was audited and flawed samples were removed. This is a smaller-but-cleaner benchmark philosophy, which is appropriate for diagnostic evaluation.

Scoring is hybrid. Closed-ended tasks use precision-oriented reference metrics such as Accuracy, while open-ended tasks use ROUGE-L and, for tasks like paragraph filling where lexical overlap is insufficient, an LLM-as-a-judge setup. That trade-off is sensible: pure reference metrics under-credit semantically correct paraphrases, while pure judge-based evaluation reduces reproducibility. MGAL uses both.

Relative to LongBench, LV-Eval, M4LE, and ONERULER, the novelty is not simply broader coverage. The main difference is tighter control over variables that matter for error attribution: multilingual alignment, discourse granularity, and evidence position.35:T8dd,The experimental section is useful mainly for the error patterns it exposes. The benchmark is evaluated on 12 long-context LLMs. The main findings reported in the paper fall into four groups.

First, performance degrades as granularity becomes coarser. Models do well on word-level tasks, but performance drops at sentence, paragraph, and document levels. This suggests that many current long-context models are stronger at local matching or retrieval than at stable discourse-level integration.

Second, multilingual gaps widen in lower-resource languages. Large open-source and closed-source models are relatively comparable on higher-resource languages, but closed-source systems maintain a clearer lead on lower-resource ones, and smaller open models degrade more sharply. For pretraining, this points to data coverage and multilingual representation quality as major bottlenecks, not just architecture.

Third, position sensitivity remains strong. In sentence cloze, blanks appearing earlier in the text produce the highest error rates, with performance improving toward later positions, consistent with recency-biased attention. The authors also observe an early-option bias: models disproportionately choose earlier answer options such as A or B, indicating reliance on answer-order heuristics rather than evidence verification.

Fourth, the paper identifies two more specific failure modes. One is local semantic crowding: when neighboring sentences share entities, years, or discourse markers, models over-rely on surface overlap and pick the wrong sentence, failing to distinguish functional roles such as background, explanation, or outcome. The other is a fluency-consistency gap: generated outputs are often fluent and stylistically plausible but weakly anchored to the source, leading to factual drift and unsupported claims.

In terms of benchmark scale, MGAL covers 6 languages, 7 tasks, 4 granularities, and contexts from 8K to 128K, with 420 examples per task per language. Even without chasing a single aggregate score, this is enough to support meaningful slicing by granularity and position. The most informative numbers are likely the relative slopes across word→sentence→paragraph→document and begin→middle→end, not the overall ranking.36:T936,UpgradeBench 的设计核心是 longitudinal，而不是静态 pairwise transfer。作者沿着四代 Qwen release 加上一条 continuation release，构造了真实的升级路径；同时补充 OLMo checkpoints，因为 OLMo 的训练关系是公开且可验证的，可以把“是否来自同一训练轨迹”当成受控变量，而不是靠 release note 猜。

它把升级决策拆成四种策略：freeze、port、refresh、retrain。freeze 是继续部署旧 specialist；port 是把旧 adapter 或 task vector 直接迁到新 base；refresh 是用旧 specialist 作为 teacher，对新 base 做便宜的再专门化；retrain 则是从头按固定 recipe 重训 specialist。围绕这四种策略，作者定义并测量了几个关键量：retention、recovery cost、specialization half-life，以及 upgrade tax。这里最重要的是 retention：新策略相对“该 hop 上可达到的最佳 specialist”保留了多少收益。这样定义后，不同 hop、不同任务、不同模型规模可以放到同一坐标系里比较。

任务设置覆盖 6 个应用任务、3 类任务、2 个模型尺度，并把训练侧成本纳入 benchmark 输出，包括 GPU-minutes 和 energy。这个点做得比较扎实，因为很多迁移论文只报最终精度，不报恢复到同等质量需要多少代价，而实际部署决策恰恰取决于这个成本。

方法上它没有提出新的迁移算法，贡献在于实验设计和变量控制。尤其是对 port 的分析很关键：作者直接检验一个业界常见假设——只要新旧模型架构兼容，adapter 原样拷贝就是强基线。实验结论是否定的。决定能否直接拷贝的不是 shape compatibility，而是训练连续性，也就是两个 checkpoint 是否位于同一权重演化轨迹上，以及相距多远。

OLMo 部分进一步把这个结论做成了更接近机制层面的测量。沿同一 documented trajectory 往后走，adapter retention 会随着 continuation token 数增加而下降；文中给出的边界是，从 46B-token continuation 时仍有 0.88–0.99 retention，走到 2.9T-token continuation 时则掉到接近 no-adapter floor。后续 anneal 和 model-soup merge 并没有带来额外主要损伤。这个结果很有信息量，因为它把“能不能 port”从模糊经验变成了 distance budget 的问题。37:T8e6,实验规模不算大，但设计很完整：总共 98 次训练、193 个 evaluation cells，全部在一张 RTX 4090 上完成，并释放了 per-query prediction、bootstrap CI 和 energy log。这个设置说明作者更关心可复现的决策测量，而不是堆大算力。

几个关键结果值得记住。第一，specialist 不一定会很快过时。对于 intent classification，freeze 在 9 个 upgrade hops 上能保留 99%–101% 的 attainable gain；跨四代模型，retrained specialist 的参考分数只移动了 0.2 分，而 zero-shot 却提升了 21 分。这说明某些任务的专门化收益主要来自标签体系、输出格式或任务数据本身，base 进步并不会立刻吞掉这些收益。

第二，另一些任务则明显更依赖 base 能力。text-to-SQL 上，冻结旧 specialist 在单个 hop 上最多会损失 59% 的 attainable gain，而且它相对新 base 的优势会在三代内衰减到零。作者据此提出 data-bound 与 base-bound 的区分：前者 specialist moat 更稳，后者随着 base reasoning 能力增强会更快被追平。

第三，naive porting 可能非常危险。文中给了一个很强的反例：在两个架构完全相同但来自独立预训练运行的 checkpoint 之间，直接贴 adapter 会把 intent classification 从 92.8% 打到 42.9%，甚至低于不用 adapter 的 60.7%。但如果目标模型是同一权重继续预训练得到的 descendant，同样的操作可以保持接近 reference-level accuracy。这一组结果基本否定了“同家族同结构即可直接迁移”的经验法则。

第四，OLMo 验证复现了同样规律，并进一步定位了边界：同一训练轨迹上，随着 continuation 距离增大，port retention 从 0.88–0.99 逐步跌到 no-adapter floor；而 anneal、model soup merge 不是主要破坏源。这个结论对 checkpoint 发布和 continual pretraining 很有现实意义。

文中还给出了一个 half-life 估计：在 7–8B 规模，specialization half-life 大约落在 2–3 个 generation；在 1.5–1.8B 规模，至少观察到一次 crossing，即新 base 的便宜采用策略真正超过旧 specialist。这个量化虽然依赖任务集，但比过去泛泛讨论“要不要升级”要扎实得多。38:T955,The main design choice is longitudinal evaluation rather than static pairwise transfer. The benchmark follows four consecutive Qwen releases plus one continuation release, creating a realistic upgrade path. It also includes OLMo checkpoints, where training relationships are documented rather than inferred, allowing the authors to treat training continuity as a controlled variable.

The benchmark evaluates four upgrade strategies: freeze, port, refresh, and retrain. Freeze keeps serving the old specialist. Port copies the old adapter or task vector onto the new base. Refresh uses the old specialist as a teacher to cheaply re-specialize the new base. Retrain rebuilds the specialist from scratch under a fixed recipe. Around these strategies, the paper defines and measures retention, recovery cost, specialization half-life, and upgrade tax. Retention is especially important: it measures how much of the attainable gain on the target hop is preserved by a cheaper strategy relative to the best specialist achievable there. This makes comparisons across hops, tasks, and model scales much more meaningful.

The benchmark covers six application tasks from three classes, at two model scales, and treats training-side cost as a first-class output, including GPU-minutes and energy. That is a strong design choice because deployment decisions depend on cost-to-recover, not just final accuracy.

The paper does not introduce a new transfer algorithm. Its contribution is experimental structure and variable control. The most important analysis concerns porting. A common practitioner assumption is that if two checkpoints are shape-compatible, direct adapter copying is a strong baseline. The paper shows that this assumption is false. What licenses copying is not architectural shape but training continuity: whether the target checkpoint lies on the same weight trajectory, and how far away it is.

The OLMo experiments sharpen this into something close to a mechanistic statement. Along the same documented trajectory, adapter retention decays as continuation distance grows. The paper reports retention of 0.88–0.99 after a 46B-token continuation, but near no-adapter-floor performance after a 2.9T-token continuation. Subsequent annealing and model-soup merging add little extra damage. This turns portability from a vague family-level intuition into a distance-budget question.39:T9cb,The experimental scale is modest but well structured: 98 trainings and 193 evaluation cells, all run on a single RTX 4090, with per-query predictions, bootstrap confidence intervals, and energy logs released. The emphasis is clearly on reproducible decision measurement rather than brute-force benchmarking.

Several results stand out. First, specialists do not always become obsolete quickly. On intent classification, freezing retains 99%–101% of the attainable gain across nine upgrade hops. Over four generations, the retrained-specialist reference score moves by only 0.2 points, while zero-shot performance rises by 21 points. This suggests that some specialist value is tied mainly to task data, label taxonomies, or output conventions rather than to frontier base capability.

Second, other tasks are much more base-dependent. On text-to-SQL, the frozen specialist can forfeit up to 59% of attainable gain on a single hop, and its advantage over a newer base decays to zero within three generations. This motivates the paper’s data-bound vs base-bound distinction: some specialist moats are stable, others are rapidly eroded by improvements in the base model’s reasoning ability.

Third, naive porting can fail catastrophically. The paper gives a strong counterexample: between two architecturally identical checkpoints from independent pretraining runs, directly pasting the adapter drops intent classification from 92.8% to 42.9%, which is even worse than the 60.7% obtainable with no adapter at all. But when the target is a continued-pretraining descendant of the same weights, the same operation preserves near-reference accuracy. This directly challenges the common rule of thumb that same-family, same-shape transfer is safe.

Fourth, the OLMo validation reproduces the same pattern and localizes the boundary more precisely. Along the same training trajectory, port retention falls from 0.88–0.99 to the no-adapter floor as continuation distance increases; annealing and model-soup merge are not the main sources of damage. This is highly relevant for continual pretraining and checkpoint release strategy.

The paper also estimates a specialization half-life of roughly two to three generations at the 7–8B scale, and observes at least one actual crossing at 1.5–1.8B where cheap adoption of the new base overtakes the old specialist. The exact values are task-dependent, but this is much more concrete than the usual qualitative discussion around whether upgrades are worth it.3a:T49b,Read this one with a concrete deployment or training question in mind. Start with the introduction and Figure 1, where the paper lays out the three intuitions it challenges: specialists do not always age quickly, porting is not always safe, and task differences are not well captured by a generative/discriminative split. Then read the benchmark setup and metric definitions carefully, especially retention, recovery cost, and specialization half-life. Without those definitions, the later conclusions can look like anecdotal observations.

In the experiments, prioritize the strategy comparisons across tasks and the evidence from both Qwen and OLMo supporting the claim that continuity, not architectural shape, governs portability. If you work on continual pretraining, checkpoint selection, adapter ecosystems, or practical model deployment, this is worth a full read. If you only need the main conclusions, 40–60 minutes on the main paper should be enough. The section around the independent-pretraining-run vs continued-pretraining-descendant comparison is the most important one to study closely, because it directly probes the limits of parameter-space transferability.3b:T5a3,The method is an agentic LLM-as-judge setup. The data consists of pairs of revisions for the same declaration from Mathlib PRs: an earlier version that was sent back for revision, and the later version that was accepted. Importantly, each version is scored independently. The judge does not know the score of the other revision and is not explicitly told it is doing pairwise comparison, which reduces comparison leakage. The rubric decomposes proof quality into five dimensions: library leverage, automation fit, structural clarity, statement quality, and Mathlib conventions. Each dimension is scored from 1 to 10, then combined by a harness into a final score. The key design choice is tool grounding. The judge can query the exact Mathlib state at the relevant commit through bash, search for existing lemmas, inspect signatures, and check available APIs. That lets it reason like a human reviewer: is this proof reinventing something already in the library, should the statement be more general, is there a cleaner one-line invocation already available. This is materially different from a text-only judge because proof quality often depends on library context, not just the local proof text. For dataset construction, the authors used Claude Sonnet 5 to inspect historical PRs and identify cases with substantial revision differences that were not just linting or newly added declarations, then split them into development and test sets.3c:T5dc,The benchmark contains 218 declaration pairs in the test split and 123 pairs in the dev split used for rubric tuning. Six judges were evaluated, including three open-weight and three closed models, with three replicates per judge. Each evaluation allows up to 20 tool calls per sample to cap inference cost. The main metric is preference alignment: whether the judge assigns a higher score to the revision that Mathlib ultimately accepted. All models perform well above the 50% random baseline. Claude Sonnet 5 is best at 80.8%, while even DeepSeek-V4-Flash reaches 63.5%, with all sign tests reported at p < 1e-5. Among open models, muse-glimmer-30b gets 70.2% and inkling-small 69.3%, showing that the task is not exclusive to frontier closed models. The most useful experimental finding is the variance analysis. Re-running the same judge on the same declarations flips roughly one-fifth to nearly half of the verdicts. Some ablations look non-significant in one replicate, p = 0.34, and significant in another, p = 0.006. That means single-run judge benchmarks can easily overstate differences. The PR 11640 case study is also informative: the initial proof reconstructed a result already present in Mathlib, while the merged revision replaced it with a one-line call to H.restrictPreimage s. The judge found the existing declaration via tool use and consistently penalized the initial version on library leverage. This kind of semantic review cannot be replicated by linting or regex heuristics.3d:T736,The method, NAPE, starts from a log-mel spectrogram, splits it into non-overlapping patches, and projects each patch into a latent embedding. This yields a 2D grid of patch embeddings. Since the backbone is a causal Transformer over a 1D sequence, the grid must be serialized with a scanning order. The model then predicts the embedding of the next patch from the prefix, using a lightweight predictor head. Training uses negative cosine similarity to a stop-gradient target embedding, rather than L2 regression or classification over discrete acoustic tokens.

The most interesting technical point is that scanning order is treated as a first-class design choice. In images, rasterization is often a convenience. In audio spectrograms, time and frequency axes play very different roles, so the serialization order changes both the available causal context and the inductive bias. The paper studies four scanning strategies and shows that this choice materially affects transfer performance and training behavior. The trade-off is intuitive: if the order destroys temporal continuity, the model no longer follows natural acoustic evolution; if it only marches along time, it may underuse local time-frequency structure. Making this axis explicit is one of the paper’s main contributions.

Architecturally, the setup is deliberately minimal: a single causal encoder plus a predictor head, with no teacher branch, no EMA target network, no reconstruction decoder, and no extra variance/covariance regularization. This is the key difference from much of prior audio SSL. The model is not trained to reconstruct masked content or to match a teacher representation. It is trained to model audio as a sequential latent process, which makes it much closer in spirit to language modeling and easier to align with unified multimodal pretraining recipes.3e:T5a7,Pretraining is done on AudioSet, with downstream evaluation on ESC-50, Speech Commands V1/V2, and IEMOCAP. The paper reports three model sizes: Small (19M), Base (85M), and Large (303M), mainly to test whether the objective scales. According to the full text, NAPE reaches state-of-the-art or near-state-of-the-art performance on several tasks, and importantly it also performs well under linear probing, not just full fine-tuning. That matters because next-embedding prediction does not explicitly optimize for linear separability.

The ablations are more informative than the headline numbers. The paper studies scanning order, model scale, and objective/architecture choices. The main takeaways are that scanning order matters a lot, temporally aligned serialization is usually stronger and more stable, and performance improves consistently from Small to Large without obvious breakdown of the objective. The fact that this works without a decoder, tokenizer, or teacher is itself an empirical result. The qualitative analyses, including attention maps and embedding-space structure, suggest that the model captures meaningful acoustic boundaries and local spectro-temporal patterns. The excerpt provided here does not include the full numeric tables, so I will not invent exact margins, but the paper clearly does more than present a single best result: it builds a fairly complete empirical case for simple causal latent prediction in audio.3f:T473,For a quick read, start with the introduction and the main method figure. The central point is that this is not another masked spectrogram model; it is a test of whether audio can support next-embedding pretraining directly. Then go to the section defining the scanning strategies. That is the most informative design choice in the paper. After that, inspect the main results table with two questions in mind: how close does the simple recipe get to stronger but more complex audio SSL baselines, and does the representation remain useful under linear probing? Finally, read the ablations and qualitative analyses to judge whether the model learns stable temporal-acoustic structure rather than exploiting an optimization shortcut.

If time is limited, about 30 minutes is enough to capture the main idea: method figure, scanning-order ablation, main benchmark table, and one qualitative section. If the goal is to assess relevance for unified multimodal pretraining, spend extra time on the serialization design and the cosine latent-prediction loss, because those are the pieces most likely to transfer to broader next-latent frameworks.40:T9bd,Libra 的核心设计是 decoupled vision-language system：一套 vision system，一套 language system，中间用 cross-modal bridges 连接。关键不只是双塔，而是在每层内部把 self-modal modeling 和 cross-modal interaction 分成两种计算模式。

第一是 switch attention。作者区分两种场景：模态内部建模时，用标准 attention；跨模态交互时，不直接让一种模态去对齐另一种模态的原始特征空间，而是先通过 cross-modal bridge 把 Q/K/V 映射到新的交互空间，再做注意力。这么做的动机很明确：如果直接共享同一注意力空间，视觉和语言会互相拉扯，尤其在数据不平衡时更容易让弱势模态被强势模态同化。bridge 相当于给跨模态交互加了一个受控接口，保留各自表示的自治性。

第二是 switch FFN。它对应不同任务场景下的后续变换分流，用来进一步区分理解与生成、以及自模态与跨模态信息融合后的处理方式。虽然从摘要和引言看，具体实现细节需要结合方法章节确认，但设计意图很清楚：attention 负责信息路由，FFN 负责场景化变换，二者共同完成“共享框架下的计算解耦”。

在实例化上，作者给了两个版本。Libra-1 面向 understanding-only image-to-text。这里有两个值得注意的点：一是 supervision 采用 unified discrete autoregression，把视觉和语言都放进离散 AR 框架里，优先换取训练稳定性；二是 vision input 采用 hybrid tokenization，把连续视觉信号和离散 ID 结合起来，试图兼顾细粒度感知与离散序列训练的稳定性。这个选择说明作者并不完全相信纯离散视觉 token 足够表达感知细节。

Libra-2 扩展到 unified understanding + generation。按引言描述，它在 tokenization、representation、supervision 上又加了额外策略，目标是同时支持 image-to-text 和 text-to-image。这里最关键的不是“能不能统一”，而是统一后是否还能维持两边能力不互相伤害。Libra 的回答是通过结构分流降低干扰，而不是完全依赖数据配比去硬平衡。

如果和典型 unified MLLM 比，Libra 的区别在于它不把跨模态交互视为默认状态，而把它视为一种需要显式开关和专门映射空间的计算模式；如果和 cascade 比，它又没有把视觉生成完全外包给独立大后端，而是保留了端到端联合学习的可能。41:Ta8b,Libra is a decoupled vision-language system with one vision branch and one language branch connected by cross-modal bridges. The key idea is not merely having two towers, but introducing two computation modes inside the model: self-modal modeling and cross-modal interaction.

The first component is switch attention. For self-modal modeling, Libra uses standard attention. For cross-modal interaction, it does not directly align one modality into the raw feature space of the other. Instead, queries, keys, and values are mapped through a cross-modal bridge into a dedicated interaction space before attention is computed. The motivation is clear: direct sharing of the same attention space can cause one modality, usually language, to dominate the other under imbalanced data. The bridge acts as a controlled interface that preserves modality-specific representations while still allowing information exchange.

The second component is switch FFN. It further separates computation after attention depending on the scenario, helping distinguish self-modal processing from cross-modal fusion, and likely also understanding from generation. The exact implementation details need the method section, but the design intent is straightforward: attention handles routing, FFN handles scenario-specific transformation, and together they realize decoupled computation within a unified framework.

The paper instantiates the framework in two versions. Libra-1 targets understanding-only image-to-text modeling. Two details matter here. First, supervision uses unified discrete autoregression over both vision and language, prioritizing training stability. Second, vision inputs use hybrid tokenization, combining continuous visual signals with discrete IDs to balance fine-grained perception and stable sequence modeling. This suggests the authors do not fully trust purely discrete visual tokens to carry enough perceptual detail.

Libra-2 extends the framework to joint multimodal understanding and generation. According to the introduction, it adds further strategies in tokenization, representation, and supervision to support both image-to-text and text-to-image. The central claim is not simply that these tasks can be unified, but that they can be unified without one side damaging the other, thanks to structural decoupling rather than only careful data balancing.

Compared with typical unified MLLMs, Libra treats cross-modal interaction as an explicit computation mode with dedicated mappings, not as the default state of every layer. Compared with cascade systems, it does not outsource visual generation to a separate large backend, so it preserves the possibility of end-to-end joint learning.42:T597,From the available full-text fragment, the experiments are organized in two settings: Libra-1 for multimodal understanding and Libra-2 for joint understanding and generation. The paper claims that the architecture is effective in both settings and that the evaluation also covers tokenization, representation, and supervision choices.

However, the provided content does not include the later experimental tables, so it is not possible to responsibly report exact model sizes, training tokens, dataset scales, or precise gains over baselines. What can be stated with confidence is that the paper evaluates not only understanding-only settings but also unified generation settings, which is more complete than many works that stop at captioning or VQA. The separate Libra-1 and Libra-2 instantiations also indicate an important methodological point: a unified architecture does not imply a single training recipe; understanding-only and understanding-plus-generation require different supervision and tokenization choices.

If the full experiments include ablations, the most important ones to inspect are: removing switch attention or switch FFN, comparing hybrid tokenization against purely discrete or purely continuous visual inputs, and measuring whether unified training introduces a clear trade-off between understanding and generation quality. Without those ablations, the architectural claim would be much harder to assess.43:T619,This is a skim-to-careful-read paper if you care about native multimodal pretraining rather than downstream VQA scores alone.

Start with the introduction and the architecture overview. The main point is not just building an MLLM that can generate images, but addressing training bias under modality imbalance in unified systems. Then read the switch attention and switch FFN sections carefully. In particular, check what the cross-modal bridge actually is: an independent parameter space, a low-rank mapping, or a gated projection. That determines whether the gain comes from inductive bias or simply from added capacity.

Next, compare Libra-1 and Libra-2. The important part is not the naming but how supervision, tokenization, and losses differ between understanding-only and joint understanding-generation settings. If reading selectively, prioritize the architecture figure, the tokenization design, the main result tables, and any ablations directly tied to modality imbalance. Expected reading time is about 35 to 50 minutes.

Three deeper questions are worth keeping in mind. First, whether hybrid tokenization is merely an engineering patch or evidence that current discrete visual tokenizers are still insufficient for unified AR modeling. Second, whether the switch modules help because they impose a better inductive bias or because they isolate parameters and reduce negative transfer. Third, whether this decoupled design still holds at much larger data scale, or mainly serves as a practical compromise under current multimodal data limitations.44:T50d,The analysis starts from standard flow matching. A time-dependent vector field vθ transports a simple prior p1, usually noise, to the data distribution p0 through an ODE. Standard training fits the conditional velocity along interpolation paths. FlowDPO-style methods avoid exact likelihood computation by replacing log probabilities with timestep-wise flow regression errors, then applying a DPO-like pairwise objective to winner and loser samples. The paper shows why this can be unsafe: the preference update can alter the terminal endpoint of the flow in directions that are normal to the pretrained data manifold. Optimal flow matching recovers the terminal data distribution, but a preference update leaves the manifold whenever its induced terminal displacement has a nonzero normal component. ThermoDPO addresses this by adding a temperature-controlled winner-side anchor. As the temperature τ goes to zero, the objective recovers rejection sampling fine-tuning. For τ>0, it decomposes into a temperature-scaled FlowDPO term plus a nonnegative anchoring term. This makes the alignment–preservation trade-off explicit. The weighted variant, ThermoDPO-weighted, fixes practical weighting issues near t=0 and comes with a bound on a reconstruction-based surrogate for manifold drift.45:T858,方法叫 R²-OPD，关键设计有三步。第一步，用 teacher-independent 的 process reward 估计 reasoning progress。做法不是只看最终答对与否，而是对连续推理状态采样后续 continuation，比较从不同中间状态继续生成时的 solve probability 变化，把某段推理对最终解题概率的边际贡献当作 progress signal。作者强调 raw process reward 噪声很大，原因一是 Monte Carlo continuation 采样方差，二是中间 reasoning span 的边界本身不稳定。

第二步，做 sign-consistent span merging。原始轨迹先被切成 reasoning spans，然后把 process reward 符号一致、相邻的 span 合并成更大的 segment。这样做有两个目的：一是减少对细粒度边界估计的依赖，二是让后续比较发生在更稳定的语义单元上。文中给了理论说明：如果内部边界估计有误差，合并后这些误差在段内会相互抵消；同时对 token-level divergence 做段内平均，也能在弱相关假设下降低局部方差。

第三步，用 ranking conflict 做 reward filtering，而不是 reward replacement。对每个合并后的 segment，作者聚合 token-level reverse-KL distillation loss，得到该段“偏离 teacher 的惩罚强度”；同时用 process reward 给出该段“推进解题的贡献”。如果两个排序冲突，例如 progress 更高的段反而被蒸馏损失惩罚得更重，就说明 teacher guidance 在这一局部可能不可靠。R²-OPD 不把 process reward 加到 loss 里，也不重写 OPD objective，而是把这些冲突段的 distillation reward mask 掉，只保留 progress 与 distillation 一致的部分继续监督。这个选择很关键：它把 process reward 用作校验器而不是训练主信号，降低了高噪声 reward 直接驱动优化的风险。

从实现上，论文基于 reverse-KL OPD，并采用 support-restricted 近似来计算 token-level KL，只在学生或师生联合的 top-H support 上做分布匹配，避免全词表计算。R²-OPD 叠加在这个标准 OPD 框架之上，因此兼容现有 on-policy 蒸馏流水线。46:T866,The method, R²-OPD, has three main components. First, it estimates reasoning progress using teacher-independent process rewards. Instead of relying only on final correctness, it samples continuations from successive intermediate reasoning states and measures how the probability of solving the task changes. This gives a marginal contribution estimate for each reasoning span. The paper emphasizes that raw process rewards are noisy because continuation-based estimation has Monte Carlo variance and because fine-grained reasoning boundaries are themselves unstable.

Second, it performs sign-consistent span merging. The trajectory is segmented into reasoning spans, and adjacent spans with process rewards of the same sign are merged into larger segments. This reduces sensitivity to noisy internal boundaries and creates more stable units for calibration. The theoretical discussion argues that internal boundary estimation errors can cancel within merged segments, and that averaging token-level divergence over a segment reduces local variance under weak dependence assumptions.

Third, it detects local ranking conflicts between progress and distillation penalty, and uses those conflicts for filtering. For each merged segment, token-level reverse-KL distillation losses are aggregated into a segment-level penalty for deviating from the teacher. Process reward gives a segment-level estimate of reasoning advancement. If a higher-progress segment is penalized more heavily than a lower-progress one, the two signals disagree in ranking, indicating that teacher supervision may be unreliable there. Instead of adding process reward into the objective or replacing OPD, R²-OPD masks distillation rewards on the most inconsistent segments and keeps teacher guidance elsewhere.

This design choice is the core idea. Process reward is treated as a reliability probe rather than a primary training signal, which avoids directly optimizing against a high-variance reward. The implementation is built on reverse-KL OPD with support-restricted approximation over a top-H token set, so the method can be layered onto standard practical OPD pipelines.47:T776,The experimental section is aimed at validating the signal-calibration hypothesis rather than demonstrating a large-scale model jump. The tasks are reasoning-heavy, and the comparisons include vanilla OPD, variants that directly incorporate outcome or process rewards, and alternative filtering or weighting strategies.

Three findings stand out. First, the mismatch is real: the paper provides qualitative examples and aggregate analysis showing that some intermediate spans increase downstream solve probability while receiving stronger distillation penalties because they diverge from the teacher distribution. This is especially visible on harder reasoning problems.

Second, the gains come from removing bad supervision rather than injecting another reward. Compared with directly mixing process rewards into optimization, the filtering-based approach is more stable and more consistently helpful. The explanation is straightforward: raw process rewards are noisy, so using them as direct optimization targets can distort learning, whereas using them only to suppress clearly inconsistent teacher signals is safer.

Third, the ablations support the two main design choices: sign-consistent merging and segment-level averaging. Without merging, conflict detection becomes more brittle because boundary noise leaks into the comparison. With token-level filtering instead of segment-level filtering, lexical fluctuations create more false conflicts. The paper also studies the masking ratio and shows that more masking is not always better; excessive filtering removes useful teacher transfer.
